\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{nexttrack_eager_margin}
  \caption{\textbf{Left:} the campaign's central result. The hinge's realized
  penalty at initialization, reconstructed from epoch-1 loss inflation at shared
  initialization (identical seed, so identical init and batch order). The margins
  were calibrated from the \emph{item}$\to$\emph{item} transition cosine (median
  \num{0.262}, p75 \num{0.584}, p90 \num{0.830}), but the penalty acts on
  \emph{prediction}$\to$\emph{current}, whose ceiling over training sits between
  \num{0.5843} and \num{0.70}: at $m\ge\num{0.5843}$ the penalized quantity never
  enters the hinge's support and the loss trace is bit-identical to the control at
  all \num{14} epochs, at $\beta$ up to the schema cap. \textbf{Right:} where the
  knob \emph{does} fire, eagerness and relevance are the same mechanism. Pushing
  the prediction off the current track's latent also pushes it off the region where
  the true next track lives, so there is no window between real de-eagering and
  breaching the relevance floor. The dash-dotted line marks the free on-record
  comparator that no arm here matched inside the floor.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-eager-margin}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{The refutation is expressive, not dosimetric, and
that is the whole finding: the calibration measured the wrong distribution.} The
margins were drawn from $\cos(x_t, x_{t+1})$ --- how far apart two consecutively
played tracks are --- but the hinge acts on $\cos(\mathrm{pred}_t, x_t)$, how close
the model's \emph{prediction} sits to the current track. Those distributions are
not comparable. Reconstructing the hinge from epoch-1 loss inflation puts the
ceiling of the penalized quantity between \num{0.5843} and \num{0.70}, so p75 and
p90 of the real-transition distribution lie \emph{outside the support of the
quantity being penalized}, and arms B3 and B4 reproduced the control's loss to
\num{16} significant digits at every one of \num{14} epochs. The design's hedge ---
that the realized penalty would \emph{exceed} its nominal value --- is empirically
inverted by orders of magnitude. The reason is structural: an InfoNCE-trained
prediction is a \emph{hedged near-average} over many plausible continuations, so its
absolute cosine to any single item is bounded low. \textbf{Eagerness is a ranking
property --- whether the current track's neighbours occupy the top slots --- and a
hinge on an absolute cosine cannot express it.}

\textbf{Where the knob does fire, eagerness and relevance turn out to be the same
mechanism.} At $m=0$ the lever works spectacularly on the facet --- A2 cuts
concentration \num{0.371}$\to$\num{0.134} and drives \texttt{ild} to \num{0.676}
--- while \texttt{music@10} collapses to \num{0.358} and recall to \num{20} of
\num{1431}. There is no separating window: \SI{27}{\percent} of real transitions
are negative-cosine, so pushing the prediction away from the current track's latent
also pushes it out of the region where the true next track lives, and
\texttt{mood\_coh} collapses too (\num{0.480}$\to$\num{0.222}). Already at
$\beta=3$ the absolute relevance floor is breached. The pre-registered failure mode
fired exactly as written.

\textbf{The prior is sharpened rather than retired, and the usable band is
identified.} The 2026-07-22 probe at $\beta \le \num{0.2}$, $m=0$ reported ``weak''
--- correct in band, wrong in interpretation. That setting sits just \emph{under}
the destruction threshold, and by $\beta=3$ the same setting is a relevance
destroyer. The entire usable dynamic range at $m=0$ therefore lies between
$\beta=\num{0.2}$ and $\beta=\num{3}$, a factor of \num{15}, unexplored, bounded
above by degeneration and below by no effect. The dose ladder run here was
\textbf{not underpowered; it was aimed past the target}.

\textbf{Two second-order results worth keeping.} First, demoting $H$ to secondary
was load-bearing: A2 has by far the best facets and the \emph{worst} $H$
(\num{0.003569}), because the grounding factor annihilates it exactly as the
$\num{5.46}\times$ derivative ratio predicted. Had $H$ been primary this campaign
would have read as a flat null instead of a mapped frontier. Second, the cheapest
real de-eagering on record remains an \emph{architecture} setting, not this knob:
the free on-record comparator at \texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=1$ gives
$\Delta$concentration $-\num{0.051}$ at a $-\num{0.016}$ recall cost, and no arm
here matched that inside the relevance floor --- the floor-respecting arms delivered
between $-\num{0.003}$ and $+\num{0.0002}$, and the arms that beat $-\num{0.051}$
paid $\numrange{4}{8}\times$ the recall.

\paragraph{Why the approved later phases did not run.} All three were gated on a
phase-A win, and there is none. Phase C's arms were additionally defined \emph{by
reference} to the winner's margin and $\beta$, so with no winner they are
undefined; applied literally to the arms that passed the floors, the selection rule
would have spent three runs transferring a \emph{provably inert} setting to another
family. The runner stopped rather than improvise, which is the correct outcome: the
next move is a re-aimed design, not the approved phase C. Concretely, re-specify
the penalty rather than re-tune it --- hinge on a \emph{ranking} quantity such as
$\cos(\mathrm{pred}, x_t) - \cos(\mathrm{pred}, x_{t+1})$, or an explicit
same-artist mass term over the top-$k$ --- and, by elimination, make the eval-time
re-rank reach the serving path, since with the training-time lever refuted the only
lever that works was at that moment one the serving path could not reproduce. That
last observation is what the next section acts on.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-07-30-eager-margin-calibrated-refutation.md}.}

\section{The walk surface: measuring what the product actually serves}
\label{sec:walk}
\emph{Rationale.} Every verdict up to this point --- including the crown board
of Table~\ref{tab:lb} and all three holisticness generations of
Section~\ref{sec:power-generations} --- is computed on a \textbf{one-shot
top-10}: the model sees one held-out prefix, ranks the catalog once, and is
scored against one target. But the artifact this lab ships,
\texttt{clients/infinite-playlist}, serves an \textbf{autoregressive walk}: it
picks a track, appends it to the session, re-scores, and repeats for as many
steps as the listener asks for. Those are different surfaces, and
\texttt{domain.toml} has said so in prose the whole time --- its
\texttt{[metrics]} block states the goal as ``mood-coherent, non-eager
\emph{sessions}, not guessing the literal next track.'' The stated intent and
the implemented metric had diverged. This section closes that gap: it defines
four walk-level metrics calibrated against the user's own listening, adds three
\emph{retrieval-time} controls, and runs a matched-sample head-to-head. It is
also the one place in this document where a documented refutation is
\emph{reversed} on a new objective, which is why it is reported at length rather
than folded into the campaign sections above.

\subsection{The surface mismatch, and why drift was invisible}
\label{sec:walk-mismatch}
Two mechanical facts explain why nothing above could have caught a drifting
journey.

\textbf{First, the anchor grows.} The \texttt{mood\_coh} facet of
holisticness@10 measures each candidate's cosine to the \emph{prefix} centroid
(\texttt{seq\_common.py:558}). On a one-shot evaluation the prefix is fixed and
that is a reasonable read of ``does this list stay on vibe.'' On a walk the
prefix \emph{is} the journey so far, so the reference point travels with the
model: a journey that leaves its starting region by a thousand individually
legal steps scores well \emph{at every step}, because each step is compared to a
centroid that has already moved most of the way there. \textbf{Drift is
invisible by construction} --- not under-weighted, but unmeasurable in this
formulation.

\textbf{Second, the composite is dominated by relevance.} Differentiating the
generation-3 composite at the champion's operating point gives
$\partial H/\partial\,\texttt{music@10} = +\num{0.206}$ against
$\partial H/\partial\,\texttt{eager} = -\num{0.038}$: holisticness@10 is
$\num{5.5}\times$ more sensitive to its relevance factor than to its eagerness
factor. So even the term that was \emph{meant} to price session quality is
mostly re-reporting graded relevance. This is the same asymmetry that
Section~\ref{sec:power-generations} observed from the outside, where the
grounding factor's ${\sim}0.3$ multiplier made every generation-2 value rank
below every generation-1 value; here it is quantified at the derivative.

\subsection{Four walk-level metrics, calibrated against the user's own sessions}
\label{sec:walk-metrics}
The new instrument (\texttt{tools/walk\_eval.py}) generates journeys from
held-out \emph{real} sessions --- a 5-track seed taken from the session, then 20
autoregressive steps, prefix items excluded, at most one track per artist unless
stated --- and scores them on four axes:

\begin{description}
  \item[1. Transition match (primary).] Do the walk's step-to-step distances
  look like this user's \emph{real} listening? This follows the playlist-lab
  finding directly: measure \emph{transitions}, not averages, and calibrate
  against the user's own sessions. Averaging a distance-to-centroid is the wrong
  shape --- it penalizes a legitimate genre move and rewards standing still.
  Reported as the 1-Wasserstein distance ($W_1$, lower better) between the
  journey's consecutive-pair cosine distribution and the real one, plus two
  legible reads: the median step cosine and the share of steps inside the real
  p10--p90 band.
  \item[2. Vibe and drift.] The \emph{seed-anchored} cosine: mean cosine of the
  generated tracks to the fixed seed-core centroid (\texttt{vibe}, higher
  better), and the end-quarter minus start-quarter difference (\texttt{drift},
  nearer zero better). The anchor is fixed at the seed and does \emph{not} grow,
  which is precisely the defect of \texttt{mood\_coh} that
  Section~\ref{sec:walk-mismatch} identifies.
  \item[3. Variety as credit.] Distinct artists and distinct genres in the
  journey, counted as a \emph{credit} rather than as the cost that
  \texttt{artist\_adj}/\texttt{artist\_conc} price. Cohesion is not one genre:
  different genres are welcome if they preserve the vibe, so a journey covering
  more of them at equal vibe is better, not worse.
  \item[4. Grounding.] Graded relevance (best sonic cosine, in the balanced
  musical-distance space of Section~\ref{sec:music}) between each generated
  track and what the user \emph{actually} played next in that session. This is
  the guard-rail: without it a pleasant unrelated drift wins, which is exactly
  the lesson the crown learned when \texttt{music\_rel} was added.
\end{description}

\paragraph{The calibration, and the single number that reframed the project.}
The reference distribution is the user's own consecutive-pair cosines over the
\num{5723} \emph{train} sessions of \dsid{seq-20260715-131139}:
\textbf{\num{73632} pairs, median \num{0.261}, p10--p90
$[-\num{0.192}, \num{0.830}]$, and \SI{27.0}{\percent} of real steps
\emph{negative}}. Real listening moves --- it changes direction more than a
quarter of the time. Against that, the engine the showcase actually shipped (a
single GRU as \texttt{gru.onnx} with a seed anchor of \num{0.4}) walks with a
median step of \textbf{\num{0.661}}, roughly $\num{2.5}\times$ tighter than the
user does (Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-walk-transitions}). The complaint that the
showcase felt ``flat despite lots of artists'' now has a measurement behind it:
many small, safe moves. The blend champion has the opposite failure --- its
median step, \num{0.317}, sits essentially on the real median, but its
\texttt{vibe} is only \num{0.187}, because its InfoNCE projection
(Section~\ref{sec:projection}) encodes ``what follows this,'' not ``what sounds
like this,'' so a mood anchor applied \emph{in} that projected space caps at
\num{0.44} while the same anchor in the raw PCA-192 space reaches \num{0.58}.
Table~\ref{tab:walk-arms} is the full first read.

\paragraph{$W_1$ is not pairable, and that mattered.} The distributional $W_1$ is
computed over a \emph{pooled} sample of steps, so it has no per-session value and
cannot enter a paired bootstrap --- the statistic every other verdict in this
document rests on. The first two sweeps therefore compared arms measured on
\emph{different} samples (20 sessions $\times$ 20 steps for the frontier, 12
$\times$ 15 for the dual-tower probe), and a vibe gap of \num{0.588} against
\num{0.624} is well inside what that mismatch alone can produce: nothing was
established. The head-to-head (Section~\ref{sec:walk-h2h}) therefore replaces
$W_1$ with \texttt{stride\_err} $=$ $|$median step $-$ \num{0.261}$|$, a
per-session quantity that pairs, at the cost of describing only the
distribution's centre.

\subsection{Three retrieval-time controls, and two refuted ones}
\label{sec:walk-controls}
None of the three controls below touches training. All are applied at
\emph{retrieval} time, on the scores the model already produces, which is what
makes them cheap to ship and cheap to re-tune.

\begin{description}
  \item[ANCHOR ($a$).] Add $a\cdot\cos(\text{candidate},\ \text{seed centroid})$
  to every candidate's score. It holds the geist by pulling the walk back toward
  the region the listener seeded it from. For the champion the term is
  z-normalized over the admissible candidates, because its base score is itself a
  z-blend of three legs and adding a raw cosine to a z-blend is scale-mismatched.
  \item[STRIDE ($s$).] Subtract $s\cdot\cos(\text{candidate},\ \text{previous
  track})$. It \emph{enlarges} each step without letting the walk leave the
  seed's neighbourhood, because the anchor still applies --- the two terms are
  orthogonal by design, one pulling toward a fixed point and one pushing off the
  last point. It is deliberately \emph{deterministic}: sampling would also loosen
  the stride, but the showcase ships permalinks, and ``same recipe $\Rightarrow$
  same journey'' is now a promise, so a stochastic decoder was ruled out rather
  than untried.
  \item[CAP.] A \emph{hard} per-artist limit applied to the ordering
  (\texttt{seq\_common.artist\_cap\_order}), after MMR, in both
  \texttt{eval\_from\_scores} and \texttt{rank\_topk}/\texttt{predict\_ranking},
  so evaluation and serving agree by construction. Best-effort rather than
  absolute: where a prefix's admissible candidate space is exhausted the
  remaining slots are backfilled with deferred items instead of returning fewer
  than $k$. Default \texttt{None}, so every pre-existing run and model is
  byte-identical; and because it needs no sonic vectors it is the only diversity
  lever here that ports to a thin client unchanged.
\end{description}

\paragraph{Why a cap and not a penalty --- the two refuted controls.} Both soft
anti-eager levers of Section~\ref{sec:holisticness} fail for one shared reason: a
soft penalty in score space loses to a steep relevance gradient.
\texttt{eager\_beta}, the training-time regularizer, is \emph{mis-specified}
(Section~\ref{sec:eagermargin}): it hinges on the absolute value of
$\cos(\text{prediction},\ \text{current track})$, which a hedged InfoNCE prediction never drives above ${\sim}0.6$,
whereas eagerness is a \emph{ranking} property --- whether same-artist items
occupy the top slots --- not a property of an absolute similarity.
\texttt{mmr\_lambda}, the eval-time re-rank, was additionally \emph{eval-only}
until the serving path was fixed, so the $\lambda$ frontier of
Section~\ref{sec:mmr} --- genuine, confirmed, and the first crown lever on record
--- had never been a deployable lever at all; and even fixed it measures inert on the pathological
case. On the real playlist \texttt{rock chabon ahre} the model ranks \num{59} of
its top \num{60} candidates as one artist and the next artist first appears at
\textbf{rank \num{75}}, while MMR's entire diversity budget there is worth
$\num{0.3}\times\num{0.26}\approx\num{0.078}$ of normalized score --- nowhere
near enough to pay for a 70-rank relevance drop. A cap does not compete on score
at all: it \emph{cannot be outscored}, the same property \texttt{seq\_extend}'s
\texttt{artist\_cooldown} already relied on. The showcase's own soft artist
penalty turned out to be effectively \textbf{binary} in the same way --- at
\num{0} it produced artist blocks with runs up to \num{13}, and at \emph{any}
value $\ge\num{0.25}$ every 30-step walk returned \num{30} distinct artists, max
run \num{1}, concentration \num{0.000} --- so the slider could not reach the
middle at all, and a hard cap exposed as a journey shape (Explore / Balanced /
Continue / Blocks) is what reaches it.

\paragraph{The cap on the one-shot surface, for the record.}
Table~\ref{tab:walk-cap} is the cap measured where the rest of this document
measures: the canonical split's one-shot top-10, paired on bit-identical cached
scores. It cuts \texttt{artist\_conc@10} from \num{0.692} to \num{0.123} at cap
3 and raises the generation-3 holisticness@10 from \num{0.008603} to
\num{0.033862}, at a cost of \num{73} of \num{1431} exact hits. The result that
was \emph{not} predicted is that \textbf{\texttt{music@10} goes up, CI above
zero, at every cap on both models tested} ($+\num{0.011}$ to $+\num{0.023}$):
the pre-registered failure mode --- de-eagering by degrading into generic filler
--- does not occur, because the items promoted from rank 75 and beyond are
\emph{more} musically apt to the true continuation than the same-artist tracks
they displace. The cap trades \emph{literal} relevance for \emph{graded}
relevance. On the champion plus \texttt{markov\_gate} (the evidence gate of
Section~\ref{sec:markovgate}, which suppresses the Markov leg where the current
item has no bigram support) the same cap 3 gives \texttt{artist\_conc}
\num{0.598}$\to$\num{0.113} and holisticness@10
\num{0.018699}$\to$\num{0.048063}, reproduced to every digit by three
registered confirming runs (\runid{run-20260730-235524-0ec1d-seq-blend} at cap
off, \runid{run-20260730-235524-93d38-seq-blend} at cap 3,
\runid{run-20260730-234635-a6252-seq-blend} at cap 5), and it \textbf{hardens on
the disjoint 0.64-cold split} \dsid{seq-20260718-211238} on two pre-registered
legs: $\Delta$\texttt{artist\_conc} $-\num{0.52699}$
$[-\num{0.54546}, -\num{0.50825}]$ CI below zero \emph{and}
$\Delta$\texttt{music@10} $+\num{0.02644}$
$[+\num{0.01919}, +\num{0.03368}]$ CI above zero, with the relevance gain
\emph{larger} on the colder window. Two cautions travel with it: exact recall is
the only regressing metric at every cap, and the canonical split's absolute
\texttt{music@10} $\ge\num{0.43}$ floor does \textbf{not} transfer --- the
second split's own control sits at \num{0.4295} on a legitimately colder window,
so floors must be derived per baseline and pre-registration must be on the
paired $\Delta$.

\paragraph{The anchor $\times$ stride frontier.} With the two score-space
controls in hand the question becomes whether the \emph{conjunction} is
reachable at all: a real-listening stride \emph{and} a held vibe.
Table~\ref{tab:walk-frontier} sweeps \num{24} cells --- \num{16} on the GRU,
\num{8} on the champion, all at cap 1 --- against a target window
pre-registered before the sweep: median step inside the real band's middle
($\numrange{0.20}{0.38}$) \emph{and} \texttt{vibe} $\ge\num{0.50}$. Exactly two
cells land inside it, one per engine (Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-walk-frontier}).
The structure of the grid is the finding: the two controls are genuinely
orthogonal --- along a fixed anchor, raising the stride walks the median step
down monotonically while the vibe decays much more slowly --- so the window is
reachable only with both on, and neither shipped configuration was inside it.
The champion's anchor also has to be applied in the \emph{raw} PCA-192 space
rather than its own projected geometry, which is why the frontier reaches
\num{0.503} where the first probe capped at \num{0.44}.

\subsection{The matched-sample head-to-head}
\label{sec:walk-h2h}
\emph{Rationale.} The two sweeps above each found a cell inside the window, but
on different samples, and $W_1$ cannot be paired --- so the finalists had to be
re-run against one another on \emph{identical} material before anything could be
claimed. \texttt{tools/walk\_headtohead.py} walks five arms over the same
\num{40} held-out sessions from the same 5-track seeds, at cap 1, and reports
paired bootstrap differences (\num{2000} resamples, rng \num{1337}) against the
\textbf{shipped} configuration --- the single GRU at anchor \num{0.4}, stride
\num{0} --- on five per-session metrics: \texttt{vibe} (higher better),
\texttt{stride\_err} (lower better), \texttt{drift} (nearer zero),
\texttt{genres} (higher) and \texttt{ground} (higher).

\emph{Results.} Table~\ref{tab:walk-means} gives the means and
Table~\ref{tab:walk-paired} the paired differences;
Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-walk-headtohead} plots the latter one panel per
metric, each on its own scale.

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{nexttrack_walk_headtohead}
  \caption{The matched-sample head-to-head, one panel per metric on its own
  scale --- deliberately not a dual axis, which would imply a comparability
  these five quantities do not have. Green is a CI entirely on the metric's
  better side, red entirely on the worse side, grey straddles zero. Every arm
  buys the real stride; only the dual-tower arms buy it without paying vibe, and
  \texttt{ground} straddles for \emph{every} arm.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-walk-headtohead}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} Read down the \texttt{stride\_err} column first: all four
tuned arms cut it by ${\sim}\num{0.25}$ with the CI below zero, which is simply
the stride control working --- the shipped engine's \num{0.399} error was
${\sim}\num{2.6}\times$ the next worst arm's. The interesting variation is what
each arm \emph{pays} for it. Pushing the single GRU to a real stride
\textbf{costs vibe} ($-\num{0.0409}$, CI below zero) and the champion pays far
more ($-\num{0.1327}$, CI below zero): for these two engines stride and vibe are
in tension, exactly as the frontier's gentle-but-real slope predicted. Both
dual-tower arms escape the trade-off. \texttt{latent/latent} at $a\num{0.8}$
$s\num{0.4}$ \emph{gains} vibe ($+\num{0.0297}$, CI above zero) and genres
($+\num{1.05}$, CI above zero) while matching the stride;
\texttt{latent/cummean} at $a\num{0.8}$ $s\num{0.5}$ holds vibe
($+\num{0.0013}$, straddling --- held, not traded) and is the only arm with
\emph{three} wins, adding \num{1.5} genres (CI above zero) and the only
significant drift improvement ($+\num{0.0351}$, CI above zero, i.e.\ the journey
ends closer to where it started). On the objective as stated ---
mood-holding, genre-exploring sequences --- \texttt{latent/cummean} is the
winner, and the single GRU that was actually shipped is not on the frontier at
all.

\subsection{The dual-tower reversal, and what the record already knew}
\label{sec:walk-reversal}
This is worth stating plainly, because it is the most consequential
methodological result in this document: \textbf{the dual-tower family was closed
by three campaigns on recall grounds and is the winner on the stated
objective.} Section~\ref{sec:dualtower} refuted it across the complete
$\{$\texttt{latent},\texttt{delta},\texttt{cummean}$\}^2$ view matrix; the
\texttt{cummean/cummean} arm was even flagged \emph{degenerate} for topping the
generation-1 holisticness at Recall@10 \num{0.066}. Every one of those judgements
was made on one-shot next-track accuracy, and none of them is wrong on that
surface --- the reversal is not a correction of the recall verdicts but a
demonstration that they answered a different question. Section~\ref{sec:power-stats}'s
power analysis makes the two readings compatible rather than contradictory: what
the dual-tower campaign established was the absence of a \emph{large} recall
gain, which is fully consistent with the family being better at something the
recall metric does not measure.

\textbf{The corroboration was already in the record, filed as a negative.} The
interpretability note at \texttt{predictors/seq\_dualgru.py:113} records that the
\texttt{cummean} tower ``added real linear directions (CKA \num{0.458}) but
\textbf{zero} next-item concepts tower A lacked.'' Read as a next-track result
that is dead weight, and it was written down as such. Read on the walk surface it
is the useful part: a tower whose content is orthogonal to next-item prediction
is exactly a tower carrying \emph{session state} rather than adjacency. Two
structural facts complete the explanation. The family retrieves in the
\emph{raw} item-latent space with no learned projection, so a raw-space mood
anchor is native to its geometry --- unlike the champion, whose InfoNCE
projection strips precisely the sound-similarity structure an anchor needs. And
one promoted arm reads the \emph{causal mean} of the prefix, which is
structurally a running-average signal.

\textbf{One sub-hypothesis is refuted.} The natural prediction from the above ---
that the \texttt{cummean} tower holds a vibe \emph{natively} --- is false: with
both controls off its vibe is \num{0.236}, statistically indistinguishable from
\texttt{latent/latent}'s \num{0.234}. The architecture does not supply the mood
hold on its own; it only becomes the best host for one once the anchor and stride
are applied. That is a weaker and more interesting claim than the one the
structural argument suggested, and it is the one the measurement supports.

\subsection{Deployment, and what is not established}
\label{sec:walk-limits}
The tuned dual-tower arm is now the \textbf{default engine} in the showcase,
exported as \texttt{dualgru.onnx} (\num{3.16}\,MB) with ONNX-versus-torch
parity of \num{1e-7} across every prefix length $T = 1\ldots40$ --- the
\texttt{cummean} cumulative sum was the trace risk, and it is the reason the
parity check swept $T$ rather than spot-checking it. The single GRU is kept
alongside for comparison, and the tuned anchor/stride live in the deployment
manifest rather than in code, so re-tuning is a re-bake and not a code edit. The
blend champion was removed from the showcase end-to-end: served from a Worker
its walk cost ${\sim}\SI{0.94}{\second}$ of CPU per batch of 8, roughly
$100\times$ the \SI{10}{\milli\second} CPU cap of the free plan the showcase is
deployed on, and batching cannot rescue a single step that already costs
${\sim}\SI{117}{\milli\second}$.

\paragraph{What this section does \emph{not} establish.} Four limits, stated
plainly. \textbf{(1)} \texttt{ground} straddles zero for \emph{every} arm
(Table~\ref{tab:walk-paired}), so no arm is established as better at predicting
the real continuation --- the wins are about journey \emph{shape} only, and the
grounding guard-rail confirms only that no arm degraded into pleasant
irrelevance. \textbf{(2)} One split, one seed, \num{40} sessions of \num{20}
steps; the walk metrics have had no second-split hardening of the kind
Section~\ref{sec:projection-hardening} applies to the recall crown, and the
target window itself was chosen from the first sweep's spread. \textbf{(3)}
Nothing here has been \emph{listened} to. Every claim is a geometric claim about
cosines in a PCA-192 space, and ``holds a mood'' is operationalized as
``stays near a seed centroid,'' which is a hypothesis about perception, not a
measurement of it. \textbf{(4)} The project's primary metric is
\textbf{unchanged}: holisticness@10 remains \texttt{[metrics].primary} and this
document's board remains denominated in Recall@10
(Section~\ref{sec:power-generations}). Whether the walk metrics should become
primary is an open decision, not a settled one --- and until it is settled, the
honest summary is that the walk surface is now \emph{measurable}, and on it one
refuted family wins.
\emph{Sources: \texttt{2026-07-30c-artist-cap-measurement.md},
\texttt{PROJECT-FACTS.md} (roll-up 2026-07-31b),
\texttt{tools/walk\_eval.py}, \texttt{tools/walk\_frontier.py},
\texttt{tools/walk\_dualgru.py}, \texttt{tools/walk\_headtohead.py}.}

\section{Stage-stack topology: depth refuted on both surfaces}
\label{sec:stagestack}
\emph{Rationale.} The user's question was direct: does an explicit \emph{stack} of
per-step MLP and recurrent stages beat a single GRU, on a corpus where five
consecutive architecture campaigns have returned nulls? Four arrangements were
specified --- \texttt{ann+gru+ann+gru}, \texttt{ann+gru+gru+ann},
\texttt{ann+gru+gru+gru}, \texttt{gru+gru+gru+gru} --- plus a fifth,
\texttt{gru+ann+gru+gru}, chosen by the user to test \emph{why} a leading MLP hurt
at $N=1$: Section~\ref{sec:preencoder} attributed that loss to half-wave
rectification destroying signed prefix information that a GRU's gated linear input
transform preserves, so putting the MLP on a \emph{gated hidden state} instead of
raw signed PCA should make the harm vanish. This is also the first campaign in this
arc to be judged on \emph{two} surfaces by design rather than in retrospect, and it
sets the standard the campaign after it inherits.

\paragraph{Design, and the equivalence proof that made a control possible.} Seven
runs of a new predictor, \texttt{seq-stack}, on the canonical split
\dsid{seq-20260715-131139} (\num{1431} test sessions), \SI{90}{\minute} of compute,
zero failures, zero contingency runs spent. Capacity was equalised at
${\sim}\num{1282000}$ parameters across the five 4-stage arms (mutual spread
\SI{0.445}{\percent}), and all seven counts were re-verified by instantiating the
model rather than trusted from the design. Everything but the stage string and the
widths is held: dropout \num{0.1}, InfoNCE, $\tau$~\num{0.07}, \num{40} epochs,
patience \num{6}, lr \num{0.001}, batch \num{128}, seed \num{1337}, MMR off, artist
cap off.

Bit-identity with the family's historical \num{176} hits is \emph{not available}
here and its absence is not a failure: the trainer re-seeds on entry, so handing it
a pre-built model offsets the dropout stream even when the initial weights match.
The campaign therefore gated on a \textbf{functional-equivalence proof} plus an
\textbf{in-batch control}, and every verdict is stated against that control. The
proof was run \emph{before} the registry edit and the server restart, so a failure
would have cost nothing: at \texttt{stages=["gru"]} the new model computes the same
function as the incumbent single GRU, with $\max|\Delta|$ \textbf{\texttt{0.00e+00}}
on both \texttt{forward} and \texttt{predict\_next} at prefix lengths
$T=1,2,5,17,40$ and \num{394944} parameters on both sides. The in-batch control
then returned \textbf{\num{178} of \num{1431}}, inside the pre-registered band
$[\num{155},\num{198}]$ and only \num{2} hits off the historical \num{176} ---
exactly the small offset the re-seeding argument predicts. Two further gates
mattered: metric completeness was green on all seven runs (no worker-image key
stripping of the kind Section~\ref{sec:mmr} documents), and the walk instrument
was verified against the shipped engine, whose $\Delta$\texttt{vibe}
($-\num{0.0033}$) and $\Delta$\texttt{stride\_err} ($+\num{0.0137}$) both straddle
zero against the freshly-trained control. The walk harness additionally reproduced
\emph{all ten} previously recorded means exactly and stayed bit-stable as arms were
added, which is what licenses pairing across separate invocations.

\emph{Results, the one-shot surface.} Table~\ref{tab:stagestack-s1}.

\begin{table}[ht]

No arm meets any win clause. Every 4-stage arm loses recall \emph{and}
holisticness by paired CI, and all five sit \textbf{below the mandatory
\num{0.108} absolute recall floor} (\numrange{0.0706}{0.0860}). Derived
per-baseline from the control's own \texttt{music@10} of \num{0.45519}, every
4-stage arm is \SIrange{5.0}{8.4}{\percent} below the relevance floor too, so none
is even relevance-neutral. The losses are $\numrange{2.5}{3.7}\times$ the measured
half-width: this is not a noise-band question.

walk metrics of Section~\ref{sec:walk-metrics}. \texttt{vibe} is always reported with
\texttt{stride\_err} beside it, at matched stride, per the standing caveat that a
vibe gain bought by taking shorter steps is not a gain.

\end{table}

Every 4-stage arm fires the refuted clause. D fires it \emph{twice}, also failing
the grounding floor ($\Delta$\texttt{ground} $-\num{0.0250}$ [$-\num{0.0474}$,
$-\num{0.0048}$]), and A additionally loses genre variety ($-\num{1.200}$
[$-\num{2.125}$, $-\num{0.300}$]). No arm approaches a win clause. Two robustness
checks close the escape routes. A pre-registered \textbf{stride probe} over
$s\in\{\num{0.2},\num{0.3},\num{0.4}\}$ was run for the only two arms with any
directional movement toward a walk win, and \emph{both peak at $s\num{0.3}$ --- the
cell they were already measured in} (C: vibe/stride\_err \num{0.586}/\num{0.183},
\num{0.548}/\num{0.113}, \num{0.517}/\num{0.153}; E: \num{0.576}/\num{0.155},
\num{0.540}/\num{0.109}, \num{0.516}/\num{0.174}); at $s\num{0.4}$ both lose vibe
with the CI below zero while \texttt{stride\_err} gets \emph{worse}. And the single
best arm was re-measured at the \textbf{deployed} cell $a\num{0.8}$/$s\num{0.5}$,
where it is refuted again ($\Delta$\texttt{vibe} $-\num{0.0158}$
[$-\num{0.0267}$, $-\num{0.0059}$], $\Delta$\texttt{stride\_err} straddling).

\begin{figure}[ht]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{nexttrack_stage_stack}
  \caption{\textbf{Left:} all five 4-stage arms fall \emph{through} the mandatory
  absolute recall floor, while the $\num{3.24}\times$ capacity control ties the
  anchor --- so the damage is depth, not capacity. Error bars are the paired
  bootstrap CI against the in-batch control, mapped onto the hit scale.
  \textbf{Middle:} the campaign's most interesting number. Arm B reaches the
  \emph{best} validation InfoNCE in the batch and retrieves \num{55} fewer hits
  than the control: the objective the whole family trains on and top-10 retrieval
  quality come apart at depth, so any architecture search selecting on validation
  loss would pick the wrong arm. \textbf{Right:} the two-surface closure. The walk
  read at the matched primary cell refutes the same five arms that the one-shot
  surface refutes; the capacity control ties on both. The axis does not merely fail
  to close on one surface --- it closes on two.}
  \label{fig:nexttrack-stage-stack}
\end{figure}

\emph{Interpretation.} \textbf{The axis is closed, and it is the first null in this
arc to close on both surfaces.} All five 4-stage arms are refuted by paired CI on
one-shot recall \emph{and} on the walk with the instrument gate green, and the
capacity control is indistinguishable on both. This is the sixth consecutive
architecture null on this corpus and, unlike the five before it, it satisfies the
standing rule that a one-shot verdict says nothing about the surface the product
serves.

\textbf{The user's mechanism hypothesis is refuted, and that is the most reusable
fact here.} Arm E put the MLP on a \emph{gated hidden state} rather than on raw
signed PCA, which the rectifier story predicts should remove the harm. It does not:
E reaches \num{118} of \num{1431}, ties two other arrangements, beats only the
worst, and comes nowhere near the control's \num{178}. So half-wave rectification
of the \emph{raw input} is not the mechanism --- whatever is wrong survives moving
the rectifier downstream of a gate. One run bought a clean refutation of a
plausible and cheap-to-believe story.

\textbf{The damage is depth itself, and three controls close the alternatives
simultaneously.} It is not capacity: the $\num{3.24}\times$ control ties on both
surfaces. It is not regularization or early stopping: \emph{every} 4-stage arm
trains \emph{longer} than the control (\numrange{21}{40} epochs against \num{14}),
and two of five reach a \emph{lower} best validation loss, so the pre-authorized
dropout-count contingency failed both of its trigger clauses and was not spent ---
the four-fold dropout application is exonerated. And it is not the MLP: a leading
per-step MLP at depth 4 is recall-neutral and \emph{holisticness-positive} against
the pure-recurrent arrangement ($\Delta H$ $+\num{0.00291}$, CI above zero). What
remains is the depth of the recurrent chain, and its cost is large ---
\numrange{55}{77} hits of \num{1431}.

\textbf{Bracketing beats interleaving at byte-identical capacity.}
\texttt{ann+gru+gru+ann} beats \texttt{ann+gru+ann+gru} by $+\num{0.01537}$ recall
with the CI above zero at \emph{the same parameter count by formula} --- the
tightest-controlled topology comparison the campaign could make. Interposing a
per-step MLP \emph{between} two recurrences is worse than putting MLPs on the
outside, which is consistent with the depth reading: breaking the recurrent chain
in the middle costs more than adding depth at the ends. Two further contrasts are
flat: the number of recurrent stages (2 versus 3) straddles on recall, and the
rectifier's position buys nothing beyond beating the worst arrangement.

\textbf{A leading per-step MLP is \emph{not} harmful at depth 4, which narrows
rather than confirms the $N=1$ result.} Section~\ref{sec:preencoder}'s ``leading
MLP is harmful'' was measured at $N=1$ against a single GRU; at depth 4 the same
addition is free-to-slightly-good. The pre-encoder pitfall should be scoped to the
shallow case.

\textbf{Objective and retrieval dissociate at depth, and this is the sharpest
instance yet.} Arm B achieves the \emph{lowest} validation InfoNCE in the entire
batch (\num{6.5378} against the control's \num{6.5832}) while retrieving
\textbf{\num{55} fewer hits} (\num{123} against \num{178}). Every prior
architecture null on this corpus was consistent with ``the extra machinery does
nothing''; this one shows the extra machinery does something measurable to the loss
and that it is the \emph{wrong} something. The operational consequence is blunt:
select on Recall@10 and the walk, never on the training objective. One caveat rides
with it --- B ran to the \num{40}-epoch cap with patience never triggering, so its
recall is a \emph{lower bound}, and a longer re-run is the clean test of the
dissociation (the prediction being a better validation loss and no better recall).

\textbf{The eagerness facets improve monotonically as the arms get worse, and the
floors caught it.} Every 4-stage arm de-eagers substantially (concentration
\num{0.359}$\to$\numrange{0.263}{0.318}) and raises \texttt{ild}; under a naive
holisticness-primary read some would look interesting. All are killed by the
absolute recall floor and by the per-baseline relevance floor --- the same mechanism
that has now saved four campaigns. Note that the canonical \emph{absolute}
\num{0.43} relevance figure would have passed several of these arms, so the floor
must be derived per baseline.

\paragraph{Two by-products, and the governance line.} A previously closed $N=2$
relative --- a dual tower with a pre-MLP on one side, closed on a one-shot recall
tie --- was walk-evaluated for the first time here and is \textbf{refuted on the
walk} ($\Delta$\texttt{vibe} $-\num{0.0255}$ [$-\num{0.0432}$, $-\num{0.0096}$]),
its one real gain being less drift. That is the same signature a recurrent-cell
variant showed earlier --- better stride and drift, worse vibe --- which now looks
like a general property of adding pre-recurrence machinery rather than anything
specific to either arm. Second, the grounding metric again discriminated almost
nothing, separating one arm of nine, consistent with its recorded arithmetic
(between-arm range \num{0.0060} against a per-session standard deviation of
\num{0.1417}, needing ${\sim}\num{2140}$ sessions): it is a floor that catches
degeneracy, not a ranking metric, and it was used only that way. Nothing was
promoted as champion, nothing was registered as a crown claim and nothing was
deployed; the seven arms were promoted \emph{for measurement only}, each carrying
the verbatim note ``promoted to enable walk evaluation; not a crown claim'', which
flooded the best-models group and was handed to the curation agent.

\paragraph{Two process findings worth keeping.} First, the predictor-checksum gate
fired \emph{on a file the design did not name for copying} --- it listed one module
to copy and a shared module only to \emph{verify}, and the verification failed. The
divergence turned out to be inert for this batch, but that is a \emph{post-hoc}
judgement that required reading the diff; the rule that caught it is the one worth
keeping. \textbf{Verify the checksum of every file in the family's import closure,
not just the ones you copied.} Second, the approved launch body was rejected by the
approved registry block: the design's JSON sent six hyperparameters the block does
not declare, so the first posts failed outright. It was resolved by dropping the
six keys \emph{after proving the drop is a no-op} --- the predictor's defaults supply
exactly those values and the resolved hyperparameter dict is identical either way,
verified by direct comparison --- and the registry block was left exactly as
approved.
\emph{Source: \texttt{2026-08-01-stage-stack-topology.md}.}

\section{Parallel tower banks: the $N$-axis closed on both surfaces}
\label{sec:towerbank}
\emph{Rationale.} The walk reversal of Section~\ref{sec:walk-reversal} left this
corpus with exactly one measured architecture \emph{win}, and with a stated
mechanism for it: a dual tower whose second view is \texttt{cummean} holds a mood
because it carries a \textbf{fixed reference never overwritten by recurrence}. A
mechanism of that shape makes a testable prediction --- it should be
\emph{dose-responsive}. If one fixed-reference tower buys a held vibe plus
$+\num{1.5}$ genres, then $N$ towers, enough of them on the raw \texttt{latent}
view to hold the recall floor and the rest on \texttt{cummean}, should buy
\emph{more} of the same. This campaign runs that dose curve, and it is the
follow-up the reversal itself asked for.

The vehicle is a new predictor, \texttt{seq-bank}: $N$ \emph{parallel}
single-layer bare GRU towers over configurable causal views, their per-step
hidden states concatenated and read by \textbf{one linear head}. Its design
choice is to collapse tower count and view multiset into a \emph{single}
\texttt{views} hyperparameter, so $N$ is not a separate axis that can drift out of
sync with the views. What the predictor \emph{cannot} express is as important as
what it can: it declares exactly \num{15} hyperparameters and none of
\texttt{fusion\_layers}, \texttt{fusion\_hidden}, \texttt{pre\_hidden},
\texttt{pre\_layers}, \texttt{layers}, \texttt{num\_layers}, \texttt{arch},
\texttt{bidirectional}, \texttt{residual} or \texttt{eager\_beta} is among them.
Every mechanism this document has already refuted --- the MLP fusion head
(Section~\ref{sec:dualtower}), the per-step pre-encoder
(Section~\ref{sec:preencoder}), the soft anti-eager regularizer
(Section~\ref{sec:holisticness}) --- is therefore \emph{structurally unreachable}
rather than merely unused, and a \texttt{parse\_views} guard rejects any spec
without a \texttt{latent} tower, which puts the registered
\numrange{0.058}{0.087} \texttt{delta}/\texttt{cummean} pathologies out of reach
as well.

Two hypotheses were pre-registered. The primary one is the \emph{dose}
hypothesis above. The independent sub-hypothesis is the mechanism the dual-tower
scan had already established: if the raw \texttt{latent} view \emph{subsumes} its
siblings, then $N$ identical \texttt{latent} towers should buy nothing, whatever
$N$ is.

\paragraph{Design and the two surfaces.} Ten runs on the canonical split
\dsid{seq-20260715-131139} (\num{1431} test sessions, cold-item rate
\num{0.5507}), \SI{84.2}{\minute} of compute, zero failures. Everything but
\texttt{views} / \texttt{hidden} / \texttt{dropout} is held: \texttt{loss}
InfoNCE, $\tau=\num{0.07}$, \num{40} epochs, patience \num{6}, lr \num{0.001},
batch \num{128}, seed \num{1337}, MMR off, artist cap off. \textbf{Capacity is
matched at \num{3.00}$\times$ the h256 baseline} for every bank arm \emph{and}
for the single-GRU control C1 (h512), with the counts re-verified from the
artifacts rather than trusted from the design --- \texttt{seq\_bank} emits
\texttt{n\_params} into \texttt{metrics.json}, and the mutual spread across the
seven \num{3.00}$\times$ arms including C1 is \SI{0.405}{\percent}. The decision
rule was written on \emph{both} surfaces before launch, and both legs are
load-bearing:
\begin{description}
  \item[S1, one-shot recall@10.] A win requires a paired $\Delta$ above zero
  against \emph{both} the h256 anchor C0 \emph{and} the parameter-matched single
  GRU C1. Two mandatory floors apply: an absolute recall floor
  $\ge\num{0.108}$ (\num{155} of \num{1431}) and a per-baseline relevance floor
  (paired $\Delta$\,music@10 vs C0 must not fall below zero).
  \item[S2, the generated walk.] \num{40} held-out sessions $\times$ \num{20}
  steps at artist cap 1, paired against the \emph{in-batch} C0, on the walk
  metrics of Section~\ref{sec:walk-metrics}. The primary cell is anchor
  \num{0.4} / stride \num{0.3}; a saved definition additionally requires
  \textbf{the same sign at the deployed cell} $a\num{0.8}$ / $s\num{0.5}$.
\end{description}

\paragraph{The instrument was pinned before anything was measured.} All ten
pre-registered gates came back green, and three of them are worth recording
because they make the comparison unusually tight. A \emph{functional-equivalence}
gate showed \texttt{seq-bank} to be a strict generalization of both incumbents
rather than a new family: at \texttt{views="latent"} it reproduces
\texttt{SeqNextLatent} and at \texttt{views="latent+latent"} it reproduces the
\texttt{fusion\_layers}${}=0$ \texttt{DualTowerNextLatent}, with
$\max|\Delta|$ \textbf{\texttt{0.00e+00}} on both \texttt{forward} and
\texttt{predict\_next} at prefix lengths $T=1,2,5,17,40$ and identical parameter
counts on both sides; an extra training-stream check matched per-epoch train
\emph{and} validation loss to the last digit over three epochs and all ten
state-dict tensors at $\max|\Delta|$ \texttt{0.00e+00}. Both bit-identity gates then
hit exactly: C0 reproduced its on-record recall@10
\num{0.12299091544374564} $=$ \num{176} of \num{1431} (a \textbf{10th}
reproduction, with \texttt{music@10}, H@10 and MRR matching to the last digit)
and R2 reproduced the dual-tower \texttt{latent/latent} f0 arm at
\num{0.1187980433263452} $=$ \num{170} of \num{1431}, so the design's band
fallback was never needed. And the walk instrument was checked against the
shipped \texttt{gru.onnx}: $\Delta$\texttt{vibe} $+\num{0.0028}$ and
$\Delta$\texttt{stride\_err} $+\num{0.0005}$ both straddle zero, so S2 verdicts
against the in-batch C0 are not an artefact of the base.

\emph{Results, S1.} Table~\ref{tab:towerbank-s1} is the batch. \textbf{No arm is
bolded as a winner because there is none}: no arm achieves even the first leg of
the win rule, all nine non-anchor arms are ties against C0, and \emph{five of the
eight bank arms lose to the parameter-matched single GRU C1 with the CI below
zero}. Both floors pass for every arm --- the worst is V1 at \num{162} hits,
clear of the \num{155} floor, and the most negative relevance $\Delta$ is V2's
$-\num{0.006109}$, straddling.

\paragraph{The sharpest number in the batch is a zero.} B4 --- four independent
h211 \texttt{latent} towers, \num{1187700} parameters --- and C1 --- one h512
GRU, \num{1182912} parameters --- return the \textbf{identical \num{186} hits of
\num{1431}}, a paired $\Delta$ of precisely $\num{0.000000}$
$[-\num{0.00839},+\num{0.00839}]$. At a matched ${\sim}\num{3.00}\times$ budget,
\emph{topology and width are interchangeable on this corpus}: whatever four
towers with different random initializations discover, a single wider recurrence
already had. Figure~\ref{fig:nexttrack-tower-bank} (left) plots the $N$-axis at
matched capacity, and the line through $\num{186}/\num{170}/\num{172}/\num{186}$
is flat inside the split's own measured resolution
(Section~\ref{sec:power-stats}).

This is also the cleanest demonstration on record of \emph{why the
parameter-matched control leg is mandatory}. Read against the h256 anchor alone,
B4's $+\num{10}$ hits would have looked like a topology gain and the five arms
that lose to C1 would all have read as ties --- the anchor cannot distinguish
``the bank helped'' from ``the extra parameters helped, and the bank spent them
worse than width would have''. The C1 leg does exactly that, and it is what turns
a batch of nine ties into a closure. Note too what the flatness costs the
\emph{other} direction: C1's $+\num{10}$ hits over C0 is itself not significant
($+\num{0.006988}$ $[-\num{0.00210},+\num{0.01607}]$), the \textbf{seventh}
parameter-matched width tie on record, so the campaign cannot claim h512 as an
improvement either despite it holding the highest single-GRU hit count measured
(\num{186} against h454's \num{179}). The single-GRU width points on PCA-192 now
read h256\,$\to$\,\num{176}, h297\,$\to$\,\num{162}, h425\,$\to$\,\num{173},
h454\,$\to$\,\num{179}, h512\,$\to$\,\num{186}, h537\,$\to$\,\num{174} ---
non-monotone, spanning \num{0.0168}, with no trend.

\emph{Results, S2.} Table~\ref{tab:towerbank-s2} is the walk. Two arms --- B2
(\texttt{latent}$+$\texttt{latent} h334) and V3
(\texttt{latent}$+$\texttt{cummean}$+$\texttt{cummean}) --- reached a genuine
\textbf{TRADE} at the primary cell, each buying a drift improvement with the CI
above zero while holding vibe. Both then \textbf{flip} at the deployed cell: the
drift gain evaporates into a straddle and \texttt{vibe} --- the non-negotiable
line --- goes below zero for both. Under the pre-agreed rule, \textbf{no
definition was saved}.
